Mara had one knee on the seat, her running number upside down and her hair escaping every pin. “There,” she said. “Now I am overdressed for espionage.”","A volunteer hurried up. The marathon had begun, minus its two most photographed entrants. The delay was already a funding slowdown. Cassian sent Rowan to explain; his own involvement required staying with the archive box. Knox's intimidation had become a claim that the museum owed him possession of its equipment. The director requested the contract before anybody attempted a private raid on the office.","The public health team asked the band to stop. A minute's quiet helped them hear an alarm that had lag. A funding slowdown was therefore the least urgent problem. Mara saw a gull soar above the roof as smoke drifted beneath it, then turned back to hasten the exit.","This was no sightseeing ride. Knox's pushy attempt to seize equipment required a blunt answer, and the director gave it. He would lock the archive away himself. His first duty was to the collection, not to the sponsor's wish to abolish inconvenient controls.","Mara opened the ticket page in Safari again. The museum's retail receipts were routed to an account under the sponsor's control. That alone did not settle a lawsuit. It did make Knox's promised neutrality look expensive. He had withheld the authorization page from the director while telling volunteers that normalization of payments was a routine technological improvement.","A mining volunteer met the crew at the gate. Cassian matched his stride, carrying a map of the closed wing so nobody wasted time at a locked entrance.","“So the donation total, minus those deductions, may be smaller?” a volunteer asked.","“Smaller than advertised,” Mara said. “Keep the original receipts.”","The museum had wanted renewal, not revenge. Its director was determined to avoid a publicity spree that made every disagreement a crime. Still, he would not renege on his promise to let staff examine the accounts. Knox could threaten a penalty; he could not turn that threat into consent.","A hearing next week would produce a ruling on the disputed access clause. Until then, the director would maintain his own custody of the records. Knox called this rivalry. Mara called it a reasonable response to contemptible pressure. The contest did not turn on who could shout longest.","A shout came from the lateral passage. Smoke showed beneath a cabinet, and the electrician ordered the wing cleared. Mara helped a visitor scramble off a bench. Cassian had to stoop under a low beam to carry out a fainting performer. Outside, fresh air made her give an angry sniff. She had been performing a solo based on the miner's prayer when the lights failed.","Rowan returned in time to catch a child trying to plunge into a puddle. “This event is excellent self-improvement,” he told Mara. “I have acquired new fears.”","The fire crew arrived, followed by a reporter expecting the planned simulation. The director refused to restage an emergency. The historical massacre, the slaughter of workers during the raid, deserved serious interpretation. Their family's old prosecution for sedition was recorded in the proceedings; it was not a script for afternoon recreation. A visitor's rebellion against a boring tour was an entirely different thing.","Knox called his opposition sentimental. Mara handed him a damp publicity sheet. His company had advertised the artificial violence already. Its discussion of nationalisation omitted both the workers' demands and the later reform. He could not oversimplify history, sell the result and demand gratitude for the spending.","The electrician found a deliberately loose connector behind the control panel. The fan's overhaul would need an independent check. Nobody knew yet who had touched it. The crew could ventilate the gallery now, but the director suspended public access until it was safe. Prevention cost him ticket sales. Concealing the risk might cost far more.","Someone had left a prototype control box connected to a damaged socket. This was an inexcusable lapse in the handover, whether it stemmed from haste or deliberate interference. Mara refused an invented explanation: she asked the electrician to record what he actually found.","On the soccer pitch, children continued their match. Their liveliness returned as the smoke cleared. The nurse moved her sex education session into another tent. She rejected a sponsor's demand that she use sexual anxiety to market products; procreation was no sales target. She had routinely answered questions without buying anything from Knox.","The reporter began to theorize about malpractice and an invasion of private data. Mara asked her to look at the forms. There was no evidence of medical records on the tablets. The disputed information concerned ticket purchases. The director's publishing partner had an accountant on the way; scientific research staff offered temporary office space.","Knox attempted another manipulation. He offered the nurse double pay to abandon the event, then proposed to poach the volunteer coordinator. Both refused. The coordinator's mother lived on social security. She knew what would happen if fees swallowed the programme. People could not be left to starve while a sponsor called the loss an adjustment.","Cassian removed Mara's wet shoe and checked her ankle. The intimacy of his concentration silenced her more effectively than any compliment. Around them the band began the same repetitive march. Rowan threatened to stomp on its drum.","He had been making a ritual of checking her shoes since their first storm. She suspected his fussing would stem from affection even if she were dressed for a picnic. She let him finish. Nearby, a child made mischief by pretending to suck an empty hose, and Rowan confiscated it before the game could become a riot. Line","volume":"婚后外传：跑错路线的慈善赛","anchor":"chapter-0105","paragraphs":["Mara finished the marathon route on a borrowed bicycle with Cassian walking beside it. Her ankle was sound but her shoe was not. No one awarded them a prize. Rowan, arriving much later, demanded compensatory tea and accused the route of an unauthorized invasion of hills.","The museum's accountant spread the receipts across a picnic table. Knox's threat of a lawsuit had not vanished. Nor had the director's opposition to the contract. What changed was their access to numbers. Ticket spending, retail income and the sponsor's fee were now visible separately. The recorded lag in payment transfers was longer than anyone had approved.","The sightseeing visitors returned after supper. Instead of performing panic for a camera, the volunteers gave them tea and answered questions about the damaged collection.","There would be no shopping spree with the recovered money. The next instalment, minus a documented repair charge, would pay instructors first. Mara watched the figures move and hoped the new ruling would reduce uncertainty rather than inspire more manipulation. A slowdown in unnecessary purchases seemed an excellent start.","A volunteer used Safari to download the daily total while the accountant retained paper copies. Normalization of the figures could wait until every unusual deduction had been explained. The proceedings of the board meeting would record the disagreement. Involvement did not make a donor an owner, however much intimidation he brought to the table.","The director declined a celebratory raid on Knox's office. Mara admired his restraint. The museum existed for recreation and learning, not revenge. Its renewal programme would continue on a smaller scale while advisers considered the disputed penalty. They would not renege on staff wages to hide a net loss. Assuming the remaining grant arrived, nobody need abandon the autumn classes.","Cassian discreetly provided a temporary loan on terms the board could examine. Mara read them herself. He watched her eyebrow rise. “You don't trust my philanthropy?”","Their rivalry over generosity had become a private ritual. He could offer unsurpassed elegance and she could still insist on arithmetic. The day had therefore produced one useful domestic reform: he promised to shave before dinner, and she promised not to starve him by forgetting it. They both routinely broke lesser promises about punctuality.","“I trust your desire to get me alone after this meeting.”","“That is precisely why you should finish reading.”","Outside, the smoke-damaged lateral room remained shut. A full overhaul had been arranged. The director's warning about malpractice referred to unauthorized maintenance, not an established medical offence. He would not let a careless word create a second scandal. The crew had left instructions to ventilate safely during repairs. The volunteers posted a fire prevention plan where visitors could see it.","A group from the soccer match returned for the evening performance. Its liveliness steadied the exhausted staff. The singer whose solo had been interrupted wanted to begin again, but without the staged simulation of a massacre. She read the miner's prayer in her ordinary voice. Then she described his family's struggle after the slaughter, their opposition to a dangerous mine and the later nationalisation debate.","The museum's publishing partner had agreed to fund a pamphlet reproducing the original proceedings. Readers could follow the prosecution for sedition, the rebellion and the eventual reform through dated records. The director's neutrality about modern elections would not require him to conceal historical injustice. Mining families deserved more than a thrilling ride through someone else's suffering.","Knox made one last pushy appearance, demanding his publicity banner. A volunteer helped take it down. No one would stage a riot for him. He could collect his property without trying to poach more staff. Mara watched him pass the nurse's tent, where a new sign preserved the promised sexual health programme. Sex education and procreation advice would remain separate voluntary services. The technological grant for scientific research also had a named manager now; vague promises could no longer swallow it.","Rowan gave a theatrical sniff. “A dreadful loss to the kingdom of bad sponsorship.”","“Don't oversimplify,” Mara said. “Some bad sponsors are charming.”","“Unlike this one, whose charm is far behind his fees.”","A boy stooped to collect the last fallen race numbers. Cassian helped him. Another child made a scramble for a runaway ball and was caught before a plunge into the canal. Mara's heart jumped. Afterward she heard the little girl suck a breath between giggles and found she could laugh too.","At dusk the band abandoned its repetitive march for a dance tune. Volunteers began to stomp out a cheerful rhythm. The nurse had finished her shift, and the coordinator who relied on social security support had been paid. The programme's salvation was a collection of modest acts, no grand conquest. The day's difficulties did stem from a badly designed agreement; its recovery also depended on people refusing the easiest silence.","Cassian's stride had shortened to match hers. She made him stoop so she could remove a leaf from his collar. Even overdressed in his borrowed museum blazer, he looked outrageously pleased with himself. Mara decided that was a form of mischief she would always forgive. Above the quay she watched gulls soar towards the last light.","Cassian drew Mara towards the quiet end of the quay. “My self-improvement resolution is to make fewer plans.”","“Mine is to buy stronger shoes.”","He kissed her, warm and unhurried, and her answer became less practical. No doubt there were still messages waiting. For now, the evening did not turn on a contract. It turned on his hand at her back and the lovely lapse between her first smile and the second."],"questions":[],"wordCount":934,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0105\" Newlyweds","volume":"婚后外传：婚旅游船的坏账","anchor":"chapter-0106","paragraphs":["The river cruise was advertised as a peaceful excursion for newlyweds. Mara discovered a football team in the lounge, a vaccination clinic on the quay and Rowan threatening to divorce a suitcase whose wheels had broken. Cassian carried it aboard without comment. His adherence to beautiful manners lasted until he saw the badminton net stretched across their cabin door.","“This is organized warfare,” he said.","Mara slipped beneath it. “Then capture the pillows.”","Their hosts, a charity supporting sustainable agriculture, had combined several events to save money. Attendance was triple the expected number. A public sector consultancy had recommended adoption of a new booking system; its default setting had quietly duplicated every reservation. The company called the problem increased utilisation. The captain used a different word.","The chairman's lofty welcome promised an enhancement of everyone's prospects. Mara hoped the endeavor would justify the speeches. She had seen volunteers toil over such an endeavour only to have its practical creation claimed by a donor. His applause for empowerment was louder than his answers about wages.","Mara hid a yawn behind her ticket. She had promised a morning workout, not a battle over beds. Cassian offered to tow the suitcases himself. A crewman explained that trucking delays had already lost the afternoon supplies. The vessel would wait at the shady river avenue until a replacement delivery arrived.","The captain had to commute from the other bank to reach his vessel each morning. The workshop could help commerce on that side, if its costs did not drive away small customers. It would be a pity to overvalue a launch party and overlook the service. Cassian thought they tended to do just that at ceremonies; opportunities to make quieter improvements abound.","At the water's edge, a fisherman argued with the organizer. Her seafood contract encouraged suppliers to overfish the nursery reaches. This threatened the reproductive success of local fish. She dismissed his concern as sweeping speculation. He asked Mara to point out the protected boundary on the map. She did. The organizer's contemptuous expression disappeared.","The contest was boats versus breeding grounds, according to the organizer. The fisherman rejected that choice. Good planning could protect both. A simple rule about where to anchor was far more useful than another brochure. Mara went to assure him his map would stay with the captain. A deckhand began to toss spare rope into a locker.","“No matter who supplies dinner, they cannot fish there,” Mara said. “Put another way, a full dining room does not remove the boundary.”","Cassian had seen problems stem from similar contracts. The fishers tended to suffer from changes made by people who never visited the river. Mara watched him negotiate a revised order. He would not let a company exploit the volunteers' enthusiasm. The terms might turn out to be defective elsewhere too.","A blast of wind snatched a folder from the organizer. Mara caught it against her chest. Inside was a census of river households, a statistical classification of incomes and the charity's claim for an enhancement grant. A third-party evaluation had identified a breach: the same equipment appeared under two funding schemes. A clawback could triple the shortfall.","The organizer grabbed for it. Cassian moved between them without violence. She called the folder private. A lawyer waiting for the cruise called it evidence of a possible tort, not a reason to start physical combat. She had joined the charity's advocacy programme to help families, and she had already asked about the missing machines.","The lawyer disliked vengeance as a substitute for practical counteraction. She asked everyone to agree on one countermeasure: preserve the documents. An allegation of tort was serious enough without a hasty accusation. It would do a disservice to the volunteers if angry language overwhelmed the work.","“It was a blunder,” the organizer said.","“Then explain the usage figures,” Mara replied.","The woman hurried below. Rowan gave a theatrical gasp at the glimpse of her vanishing shoes. He had worked hard to lubricate the afternoon with jokes. Now his supply was failing.","The fisherman told them the problem might stem from a storage contract. Equipment for an excavation of an old river shrine had been diverted through the same warehouse. Its disappearance threatened both projects. The charity's collaboration with conservators had become a story of exploitation. Mara could almost hear her mother's opinion of that.","The captain refused departure. His veto brought protests from passengers eager for the cruise. He would not yield. He had once seen a poorly secured load cause destruction; an uncertain manifest was no small inconvenience. The crew began checking the hold.","Cassian bent close to Mara. “We can still run away from this.”","“On our peaceful excursion?”","He kissed her temple. “I withdraw the proposal.”","A steward carried boiled eggs to the delayed passengers. Above him, a comedian rehearsed a joke about political treason. Rowan asked whether bad comedy qualified. The lawyer said no, and neither did a disagreement with the charity. She wished people would stop making sweeping accusations when practical questions would do.","A young volunteer, anxious about the missing grant, called the scandal professional suicide. Mara stopped his spiral with a cup of tea and a task: compare the signed delivery slips. Revenge would not restore his work. Clear records might.","From below came a scrape, then a shout. Cassian caught the bowline; Mara leapt to the quay and looped it around a post. A crewman came to tow the little boat back, looking less amused than the football team on the upper deck. Rowan offered the passengers a yawn. “An ordinary cruise. Extra rescue included.”","The captain's veto remained firm. Nobody would sail until the hold was safe. The fireman had found fuel stored beside an electric heater. This usage of the compartment violated the operator's own instructions. Its promised utilisation as a mobile workshop had never included loose cans. The organizer's request to overlook it could not yield a safe departure.","Trucking delays had turned an innocent inconvenience into an expensive argument. The attendance list showed how badly the default bookings had distorted the numbers. Mara used a phone to download the original passenger file. Its disappearance would make it much harder to distinguish error from subsequent alteration.","Mara began the workout she had missed by carrying boxes out. Cassian helped. A blast of river wind caught her scarf, and he caught its end before it met the heater. The effort brought them chest to chest. “You are distracting the rescue crew,” she whispered.","“The rescue crew is married to you.”","The lawyer watched them with open envy, then called for the paperwork. The charity's consultancy had insisted its new system ensured compliance. A file labelled ENFORCEMENT contained only a blank page. The director's lofty pledge of empowerment had not given volunteers authority to refuse unsafe deliveries. They could not divorce responsibility from control forever.","A warning board had been painted over in the renovation. The contemptuous claim that rules were for frightened people had invited this damage. The crew should not have to combat both bad storage and violence from frightened passengers. Outside, the shady avenue was filling with onlookers. The captain asked them to leave a clear emergency route.","On the quay, the fisherman unpacked sandwiches. The charity's unsustainable seafood order had been cancelled. Overfish this stretch now, he warned, and reproductive damage would be visible next season. Mara asked him to point out where temporary markers had been removed. His answer matched a photograph taken by a school group.","“No matter how hasty the mistake, the markers need replacing,” she said. “Put another way, waiting for an argument to end will not guide boats.”","The captain sent a crew to replace them. Problems tended to stem from neglect of little boundaries, he said. They might turn out much larger later. He refused to compete with the organizer's angry voice, and this seemed to enrage her more than shouting would have.","The team's collaboration now had a practical aim: locate the missing excavation tools. Mara went ashore with a volunteer while Cassian checked delivery numbers. In a shady shed they found sealed crates under a tarpaulin. The promised debut of a river restoration project had been postponed because these tools were supposedly unavailable. Someone had planned to sell them instead.","False choices abound in funding disputes, Mara thought: publicity versus responsibility, as though one cancelled the other. People tend to do better work when given time to check it. The tools might turn out to be safe after inspection, but no hurried photograph could establish that. She asked the volunteer to capture the damaged seals clearly.","“Such an endeavor,” the volunteer said miserably. “We spent a year on it.”","“Then our endeavour today is to bring the equipment back,” Mara answered. “Start with photographs. Do not lift that broken pallet.”","They found a borrowed trolley, then stopped when its clutch jammed. The volunteer would not make a second hasty decision to erase the first. Third-party records would establish ownership. Mara called the charity trustee and sent copies; she would not let the organizer drive away with everything while they admired their own restraint.","At the vessel, the organizer announced that the clinic must close. She claimed its vaccination staff had failed to pay a pitch fee. A nurse produced the signed waiver. This small act of vengeance would not stand. A retired teacher told the young volunteer to stop calling his career suicide. He had identified a problem and helped contain it. That was a feat worth being proud of.","One passenger objected to the charity's sweeping collection of household details. Its census had asked for far more information than the grant required. Calling it statistical research did not answer who could see the results. The lawyer began an evaluation of the forms. Potential tort claims belonged with qualified advisers, she said; the queue did not need a trial beside its boiled eggs.","A performer tossed a foam crown to a child and announced a game of pretend treason against the captain. The captain offered a mock sentence: triple helpings of washing-up. The laughter relieved the gathering tension. People could enjoy play without turning their real dispute into warfare.","Mara finally sat down beside Cassian. Her shoulder touched his. “I suspect dinner will be late.”","An old rope had left Cassian's palm red where he had held it in his clutch. She ran a fingertip lightly across the mark. The captain promised a new line; nobody wanted frayed fibre to infect a cut. Cassian promised, more tenderly, to run away from the next floating fundraiser with her.","“I have secured two sandwiches.”","She looked at him with such undisguised love that he almost surrendered both.
